NOTICE OF MEETING FOR RIVERHEAD TOWN BOARD  
 
PLEASE TAKE NOTICE that the Town Board Meeting scheduled for September 1, 2026 at 
2:00 p.m. at Riverhead Town Hall, 4 W Second St, Riverhead, New York shall proceed as scheduled.     
PLEASE TAKE FURTHER NOTICE, that in addition to in person attendance, the public 
can watch the September 1, 2026 meeting of the Town Board via the following methods:  
1. Live stream; namely, by viewing it on your television on Channel 22;  
2. Logging on the Town of Riverhead website, www.townofriverheadny.gov and clicking 
on the link “Channel 22” on Quick Links on the home page or by connecting to the URL, 
which is as follows:  
https://videoplayer.telvue.com/player/BjiipOg61Ac-YpNM5RFZy8f49fIMR7Kq/home  
3.  Zoom Meeting, log in as follows: 
https://us02web.zoom.us/j/88693641449?pwd=a5qoKTEs791Es0539igQvBd0VUWZCg.1 
Webinar ID:   886 9364 1449            Password:    693747             Dial by your location: (929) 205-6099 
PLEASE TAKE FURTHER NOTICE that the public hearings subject to participation via 
Zoom are as follows: 
2:00 PM - Public Hearing for the purpose and relation to the proposed increase and improvement of 
facilities of Riverhead Ambulance District, consisting of the construction of a new ambulance building 
to be located on the same site, to wit: 1111 Osborne Avenue, Riverhead, NY 11901 (Ambulance 
District Owned Property) SCTM# 600-108-2-5; and 
 
2:00 PM - Public Hearing to consider a proposed sewer connection extension at 840 Old Country 
Road, Riverhead, NY 11901 SCTM# 600-82-03-9.1; and 
 
2:05 PM - Public Hearing for the purpose of conducting the “Monroe Balancing Test” pursuant to the 
New York State Court of Appeals decision in Mtr. of County of Monroe, 72 NY2d 338 (1988), to 
determine whether, and to what extent, the Suffolk County Water Authority’s proposed “North Fork 
Transmission Line” comprising a watermain extension from Flanders to Southold, New York, may be 
exempt from the zoning and land use regulations of the Town of Riverhead relating to the Suffolk 
County Water Authority determination of the Town of Riverhead. 
 
PLEASE TAKE FURTHER NOTICE, that upon entering the Zoom meeting, you will be 
placed in a virtual audience. From there, you may either passively watch the public hearing 
live, or communicate to the moderator/host your request to make a comment during the 
public comment section for PUBLIC HEARINGS, RESOLUTIONS and/or OPEN 
COMMENTS. To alert the moderator/host of your desire to be heard, use the “CHAT” or 
“RAISE HAND” feature, then use the “CHAT” feature to inform the moderator/host of the 
following: 
1) Name; 2) Hamlet; 3) Public Hearing, Resolution and/or Open Comment Topic. 
 
Page 6 of 284
If you are calling in, please dial *9 to “raise your hand”, so we can acknowledge your request 
to speak. When it is your turn to speak, your status will be temporarily changed so you can be 
unmuted and visible for the three-minute comment period. After three minutes, you will be 
placed back in the audience. 
 
Comments/questions will be limited to three minutes. Please be patient; your admittance will 
be based on the volume of participants. Any problems or issues getting to the Town Board 
meeting via telephone or computer should be directed to (631) 727-3200 x655.

NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING 
NOTICE IS HEREBY GIVEN that the Town Board of the Town of 
Riverhead, in the County of Suffolk, State of New York, will meet at the Town Hall, 4 West 
Second Street, Riverhead, New York, on September 1, 2026, at 2:00 P.M. (Prevailing 
Time), for the purpose of conducting a public hearing in relation to the proposed increase 
and improvement of facilities of Riverhead Ambulance District, consisting of the 
construction of a new ambulance building to be located on the same site, to wit: 1111 
Osborne Avenue, Riverhead, NY 11901 (Ambulance District Owned Property) SCTM# 
600-108-2-5, as the existing ambulance building, including any ancillary or related work 
required in connection therewith, at the estimated maximum cost of $14,100,000, and the 
new ambulance building shall be owned and retained by the District and the plan of 
financing said increase and improvement of facilities includes the expenditure of 
$6,000,000 provided by Riverhead Volunteer Ambulance Corp, as Lessee, from private 
funding secured by Lessee, and the expenditure of other available funds, with the 
remainder of said cost in the amount of not to exceed $8,100,000 to be financed by the 
issuance of bonds of the Town, with provisions set forth in said Lease Agreement 
providing for payment of amounts from the Lessee to the Lessor sufficient to pay debt 
service on the bonds;   
At said public hearing, the Town Board will hear all persons interested in 
said subject matter thereof. 
Dated:  August 4, 2026 
  Riverhead, New York

Page 8 of 284
 
 
WHEREAS, a petition has been filed by Peter Moutafis, the owner of parcel located at 
840 Old Country Road, Riverhead, New York, requesting that the proposed retail cannabis 
dispensary development at the site (SCTM No. 0600-82-03-9.1) be served by the Riverhead 
Sewer District; and 
 
WHEREAS, the proposed development is not located within the existing boundaries of 
the Riverhead Sewer District; and 
WHEREAS, a map and plan dated April 8, 2025 have been prepared by H2M, consulting 
engineers to the Riverhead Sewer District, detailing the necessary measures and costs 
associated with construction required to the lateral sewer mains for the proposed development; 
and 
WHEREAS, the project will include the construction of (1) 6,060 square foot metal 
building along the north side of the existing 8,368 square foot masonry building. The proposed 
buildout of the subject site will result in a total combined floor area of 14,428 square feet, with an 
estimated wastewater flow of 536 gallons per day; and 
WHEREAS, the map and plan are available for review and inspection at the Office of the 
Riverhead Town Clerk, 4 West Second Street, Riverhead, New York, during normal business 
hours; and 
WHEREAS, said map and plan call for the construction of a privately-owned sewer 
connection through adjacent parcel to the west to existing sewer facilities on Ostrander Avenue 
north of County Road 58. The connection is to be constructed in conformance with Riverhead 
Sewer District standards and installed by a licensed and bonded drainlayer; and 
WHEREAS, all costs associated with this lateral shall be borne by the petitioner and the 
petitioner will be required to pay no net nitrogen fees based on Suffolk County Health 
Department flow in the amount of $10,720.00 (536 gpd x $20.00/gallon per daily flow); and 
WHEREAS, the Town Board desires to call a public hearing on the petition to extend the 
boundary of the district to include parcel located at 840 Old Country Road, Riverhead, New York 
(SCTM No. 0600-82-03-9.1). 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk be and is hereby 
authorized to publish and post a Notice of Public Hearing to be held on the 1 st day of 
September, 2026 at 2:00 p.m. at the Riverhead Town Hall, 4 West Second Street, Riverhead, 
New York, to hear all interested persons with regard to the adoption of a final order concerning 
the petition to provide sewer services to proposed retail cannabis dispensary to be located at 
840 Old Country Road, Riverhead, New York (SCTM No. 0600-82-03-9.1), at the sole cost of 
the petitioner and at no cost to the Sewer District and the payment by petitioner of required no 
net nitrogen fees; and be it further, 
Page 9 of 284
net nitrogen fees; and be it further,
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk be and is hereby authorized to publish and post a copy 
of this resolution in full in the August 27, 2026 edition of The News Review; and be it further, 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a Certified copy of this 
resolution to the Sewer District Superintendent and the Office of the Town Attorney; and be it 
further,

obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.

RATIFIES BUDGET ADJUSTMENT FOR AIR CONDITIONING UNITS REPLACEMENT AT JUSTICE 
COURT AND POLICE DEPARTMENT 
 
Councilman Rothwell offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Kern 
 
          WHEREAS, an air conditioning unit for the Town of Riverhead Justice Court as well as 
one unit for the Police Department, both located at 210 Howell Avenue, Riverhead, NY, have 
been deemed irreparable and require emergency replacement; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Engineering Department requested a budget transfer utilizing funds from 
the General Fund’s available fund balance to cover the estimated cost of two (2) new air 
conditioning units, including installation. Now, therefore be it 
 
RESOLVED, that the Supervisor be, and is hereby authorized to ratify the following 
budget adjustment; 
  
        FROM                TO 
 
A01-9999-000-00000-0 Appropriated Fund Balance         $60,000.00 
 
A01-1-1620-402-000-00000 Town Hall – R&M – Buildings             $60,000.00 
 
And be it further 
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to forward a copy of this resolution 
to finance@townofriverheadny.gov, Justice Court, Police Department and the Engineering 
Department; and be it further 
 
RESOLVED, all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this resolution 
from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same may be obtained from 
the Office of the Town Clerk.

  Re: Appraisal of Real Property Owned by Delalio Sod Farms, LLC 
    Located 4087 Sound Avenue, Riverhead, NY 
    S.C.T.M. #600-18-2-9.2 
 
 
Dear Ms. Hurley: 
 
 At your request, I have conducted a preliminary investigation of the above-referenced 
property for the purpose of submitting a proposal to determine the value of a portion of its 
development rights/Conservation Easement. 
 
 The subject consists of a parcel of land with an area of 155.9± acres (to be determined).  
The property is in use for agricultural purposes, specifically as a sod farm.  The site is improved 
with various agricultural buildings.  The parcel has been zoned APZ (Agricultural Protection 
Zone) by the Town of Riverhead.  The property has all development rights intact.  
 
The appraisal will, first, determine the value of the entire subject property.   
 
As per your request, the appraisal will consider the subject, in the “After” situation 
under various scenarios.  The first scenario will consider the value of the subject’s development 
rights considering the owner retaining an 18± acre area along the northerly section of the 
property.  The second scenario will determine the value of the subject’s development rights 
considering the owner retaining a 3.04± acre development area located at the northwest 
corner of the property.  A separate value will be put forth for the 3.04± acre development area. 
   
Page 16 of 284
 
 LAWRENCE INDIMINE CONSULTING CORP. 
 REAL ESTATE APPRAISALS AND CONSULTING 
Danielle Hurley, Deputy Town Attorney     August 25, 2026 
The appraisal will not consider whether there is an enhancement to other properties 
owned by the seller(s).   
 
My research will include an investigation with all appropriate agencies, including the 
Town of Riverhead, County of Suffolk, etc., in or to determine the subject’s highest and best 
use. 
 
 This appraisal will not be prepared to the appraisal requirements of the Internal 
Revenue Service or the “Yellow Book” requirements for a federal land acquisition.  This 
appraisal will not be prepared to the appraisal requirements of the State of New York 
Department of Parks and Recreation.  The appraisal will comply with appraisal requirements for 
the Town of Riverhead and the County of Suffolk.     
 
 After considering all of the above, I propose to conduct the necessary research and 
prepare an appraisal to the specifications of the Town of Riverhead for value of the subject’s 
development rights, under the aforesaid scenarios, and deliver an electronic copy and one (1) 
bound copy of said appraisal for a fee of $8,500. 
 
 Based upon my current workload, I anticipate that I can complete the appraisal within 
14 weeks of being authorized to proceed.  
 
 Should you have any questions or would like to discuss this matter, please do not 
hesitate to contact my office.  Thank you for this opportunity to serve you. 
 
        Sincerely,    
 
 
 
 
        Lawrence M. Indimine, MAI 
 N.Y.S. Certified General 
        Real Estate Appraiser #46-21925

− Successfully completed R-1, R-2, G-1, G-2, G-3 and the Ethics and Standards of 
Professional Appraisal Practice courses. 
− Successfully completed numerous Appraisal Institute courses including Advanced 
Income Capitalization (Course 510), Highest and Best Use and Market Analysis (Course 
520), Advanced Sales Comparison and Cost Approaches (Course 530), Report Writing 
and Valuation Analysis (Course 540) and Advanced Applications (Course 550). 
− Attended numerous seminars and lectures on real estate appraising. 
− Attended a class required to complete appraisals for federal transactions (Yellow Book). 
− Successfully completed the Valuation of Conservation Easement Certificate Program. 
− Successfully passed the Appraisal Institute’s General Comprehensive Examination. 
 
Page 20 of 284
 
 LAWRENCE INDIMINE CONSULTING CORP. 
 REAL ESTATE APPRAISALS AND CONSULTING 
QUALIFICATIONS OF THE APPRAISER (CONTINUED) 
LAWRENCE M. INDIMINE, MAI 
 
EXPERIENCE: 
 
Lawrence M. Indimine has been actively involved in the appraisal of real property since 1991.  
He has prepared appraisal reports for such agencies as the Federal Government, State of New 
York (various agencies including Department of Transportation and Department of 
Environmental Conservation), County of Suffolk (various agencies including Department of 
Planning Division of Real Estate), County of Nassau (County Attorney and Assessment Review 
Commission), Peconic Land Trust, The Nature Conservancy, Town of Smithtown, Town of 
Southampton, Town of East Hampton, Town of Riverhead, Town of Shelter Island, Town of 
Southold, Town of Islip, as well as other municipalities, various attorneys, individuals and 
lending institutions.  He has been tested and qualified as a fee appraiser by the State of New 
York.  He has appraised commercial, industrial and residential properties in Suffolk and Nassau 
Counties and the City of New York.  Extensive experience in appraising single-family and multi-
family dwellings.  Has testified as an expert in real estate in various courts and municipalities.  
Mr. Indimine has extensive experience preparing tax certiorari appraisals and is on the 
approved appraiser list of the County of Suffolk, County of Nassau and numerous other 
municipal agencies.  
Mr. Indimine has completed in excess of 1,000 appraisals for condemnation in Suffolk County, 
Nassau County and the City of New York.  He has testified as an expert in various courts for the 
State of New York (including the Court of Claims), the County of Suffolk and the County of 
Nassau. 
Mr. Indimine has previously prepared appraisals for properties located at Republic Airport in 
Farmingdale, MacArthur Airport in Islip Township, Francis S. Gabreski Airport in Westhampton 
Beach, East Hampton Airport in East Hampton Township and Montauk Airport in Montauk.   
 
RECENT ASSIGNMENTS: 
 
Currently working on the FIMP (Fire Island to Montauk Point) Dune Restoration Project.  
Prepared appraisal for FIMI (Fire Island to Moriches Inlet) Dune Restoration project for the 
County of Suffolk, State of New York and Army Corp. of Engineers.  Completed a Mass Appraisal 
for the Town of Southampton for the Critical Wildlands Sending Area. Completed a study in 
order to determine a temporary equalization rate for the Riverhead School District in the Town 
of Southampton.  Prepared a Development Credit Valuation for the Town of Southampton and 
tax certiorari appraisals in various locales in Suffolk County and Nassau County.  He has 
completed numerous condemnation projects for the State of New York and County of Suffolk. 
He has prepared numerous valuation/consulting studies including the impact of transmission 
lines on property values.

TB Resolution 2026-817 
 
ACCEPTS DONATION OF T-SHIRTS FOR ANNUAL SNAPPER TOURNAMENT 
 
Councilwoman Waski offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Rothwell 
 
WHEREAS, Jonathan Garcia of High Quality Transfers LLC, who resides at 41 Michaels 
Lane, Wading River, NY 11792, donated 110 Snapper Tournament Participant T-shirts, on 
August 20, 2026 for the Town of Riverhead Recreation Department’s Annual Snapper 
Tournament scheduled for September 12th, 2026, on the Peconic Riverfront; and 
WHEREAS, The Town of Riverhead Recreation Department organizes the annual 
Snapper Tournament in order to engage both youth and adult anglers, while supporting the 
Recreational Scholarship Fund. Local merchants contribute additional items and prizes to 
benefit the scholarship fund; and 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law, § 64(8), the Town Board is expressly authorized to 
accept a gift “. . . for any public use, upon such terms or conditions as may be prescribed by the 
grantor or donor and accepted by said town, and provide for the proper administration of the 
same”; and 
WHEREAS, the Town Board finds that acceptance of these gifts, at no cost to the Town, 
is in the best interest of the residents of the Town of Riverhead. Now, therefore, be it  
RESOLVED, that the Town of Riverhead hereby accepts and expresses gratitude for the 
generous donation of 110 Snapper Tournament T-Shirts from Jonathan Garcia of High Quality 
Transfers LLC; and be it further, 
RESOLVED that a copy of this resolution shall be forwarded to the Finance Department 
(finance@townofriverheadny.gov) and Recreation Department; and be it further, 
RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same may be 
obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk. 
 
 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: Adopted 
MOVER: Councilwoman Joann Waski 
SECONDER: Councilman Kenneth Rothwell 
AYES: Jerry Halpin, Kenneth Rothwell, Robert Kern, Denise Merrifield, Joann Waski 
NAYS: None

TB Resolution 2026-819 
 
RATIFIES THE AUTHORIZATION FOR POLICE DEPARTMENT EMPLOYEES TO ATTEND A TRAINING 
 
Councilman Kern offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Merrifield 
 
          WHEREAS, FEMA Center for Domestic Preparedness provides a Hazardous Materials 
Technician (HMT) five-day, forty-hour, course that prepares operations-level responders for 
Technician-level Hazardous Materials (HAZMAT) certification; and  
          
          WHEREAS, Police Chief, Edward J. Frost, recommends the following employees; 
#GFZ000983, #GFZ000903 and #GFZ000604 to attend the FEMA Hazardous Materials 
Technician Course, with all costs related including registration, transportation, meals and 
lodging to be paid for by FEMA. This training course will be held in Anniston, Alabama, from 
August 30, 2026, to September 5, 2026. 
           
          NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board hereby ratifies the 
authorization for employees; #GFZ000983, #GFZ000903 and #GFZ000604 to attend the above-
mentioned training with all costs related to the training paid or reimbursed by FEMA at no cost to 
the Town; and 
          
          BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy 
of this resolution to the Chief of Police and the Financial Administrator. All Town Hall 
Departments may review and obtain a copy of this resolution from the electronic storage device. 
 
 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: Adopted 
MOVER: Councilman Robert Kern

TB Resolution 2026-824 
 
APPROVES REQUEST FOR MILITARY LEAVE OF ABSENCE 
 
Councilwoman Merrifield offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Waski 
 
           WHEREAS, Pursuant to Article 3, section 7, of the currently negotiated contract between 
the Town of Riverhead and Civil Service Employees Association, Inc., Local 1000, which states 
that Military leaves of absence will be granted in accordance with law; and 
  
          WHEREAS, the governing law for such leave is The Uniformed Services Employment and 
Reemployment Acts (USERRA) which, in summary, provides job protection, continued benefits 
and reemployment rights providing advance notice is given to the employer, the length of 
service is cumulatively five years or less, the employee returns on time and has no disqualifying 
discharge from their military service; and 
  
          WHEREAS, current employee Donald Popino, Harbormaster I, has received orders from 
the United States Coast Guard to deploy for active duty beginning September 13, 2026 with a 
return date of September 12, 2027 and has therefore requested a leave of absence for the 
same time period. 
 
          NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Donald Popino’s request for a military leave 
of absence is hereby approved from September 13, 2026 through September 12, 2027 and will 
return as soon as practical following that date; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this resolution 
from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same may be obtained from 
the Office of the Town Clerk.

CONSULTANT/PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
 
This Agreement made the         day of                  , 2026 between the Town of 
Riverhead, a municipal corporation organized and existing under the laws of New York 
and Town of Riverhead Community Development Agency and with its offices located at 
4 West Second Street, Riverhead, New York  11901 hereinafter collectively referred to as 
“TOWN” and/or party of the first part, and H2M Architects, Consultants, Land Surveying 
and Landscape Architecture, D.P.C. (H2M), having their principal office at 538 Broad 
Hollow Road, 4th Floor East, Melville, N.Y. 11747, hereinafter referred to as 
CONSULTANT and/or party of the second; and  
 
In consideration of the mutual promises herein contained, Town of Riverhead and 
Consultant agree as follows: 
 
1.  SCOPE OF SERVICES 
During the term of this Agreement, Consultant shall provide the furnish the goods and 
services described below and more fully set forth in the attached Schedule “A” (H2M 
Pricing Proposal dated”) as an independent contractor and not as an employee of Town. 
Note, the terms of the RFQ shall apply to the scope of services, including but not limited 
to, the Consultant must adhere to the latest EFC Non-Construction, as more fully set forth 
in the RFQ. 
 
Consultant shall provide the following services:  
 
Extension Nos. 95 and 96, engineering services shall include all aspects of design and 
construction services. Extension No. 95 – River Road includes the installation of 
approximately 12,000 feet of water main along a Riverhead Town right-of-way. Extension 
No. 96 – Calverton includes the installation of approximately 14,000 feet of water main 
along Town of Riverhead and New York State rights-of-way. 
 
Extension No. 97, these services shall be limited to construction related services as the 
Town of Riverhead has already completed the design phase and received approval of the 
extensions. Extension No. 97 – Forge Road includes the installation of approximately 
2,800 feet of water main along a Riverhead Town right-of-way. 
 
The Design services include, but are not limited to coordination with other utilities and 
agencies for obtaining as-built information of infrastructure within the limits of the project, 
conduct detailed field investigation to locate all visible surface features such as road 
edges, driveways, utility features, fences, signs, etc. including existing water mains and 
marked utilities, locate underground utilities to Quality Level “B” per ASCE guidelines, 
modify existing map & plan including exhibits and cost estimate to reflect change in project 
costs reflective of grant requirements for submittal to Town Board & resubmission to New 
York State, prepare detailed design documents (plans and specifications) for the purpose 
of publicly bidding two construction contracts for each project (Installation of Water Main 
& Directional Boring Services - contracts shall include any requirements required for 
reimbursement through awarded grant including AIS, BABA, Davis-Bacon and DBE), the 
design shall include both a plan and profile of proposed water main, review documents 
with District personnel at 60% and 90% design and incorporate District comments into 
Page 58 of 284
 
 
design documents, coordinate with road agencies to determine scope of final restoration, 
prepare the necessary applications and supporting documentation for submission to the 
Suffolk County and New York State Department of Health for review and approval of 
water supply improvement.  
 
Prepare notice to bidders for use in preparing resolution to advertise project and provide 
electronic copies of contract documents for Town’s use in bid process, register project 
with New York State Department of Labor, notify DOL upon award, tabulate and analyze 
bids and submit a recommendation for award to the Town, issuance of contract award 
and provide three conformed contracts per contract for signing by lowest responsible 
bidder.  
 
In addition, Consultant shall prove assistance with applicable regulatory coordination with 
NYS Revolving Fund Equivalency checklist including coordination with Federal Agencies 
listed on Cross Cutter review check list, delineate tidal and freshwater wetlands 
boundaries, prepare and submit application to NYSDEC for approval to construct new 
water line within and adjacent to State and Federal Regulated Freshwater Wetlands and 
within the Peconic River WSRR boundary, coordination with the MTA/LIRR for crossing 
of railroad tracks incl. request for mark-out of LIRR facilities for inclusion in design. 
 
Services include, but are not limited to, the following: review submittals; review and 
respond to requests for information (RFIs); interpret contract documents; conduct periodic 
construction site visits; attend and facilitate construction project meetings; prepare 
meeting minutes; review progress of construction; review and evaluate change order 
proposals; prepare change orders, maintain logs for submittals, RFIs, change order 
proposals and change orders; provide technical support; monitor contractor’s general 
conformance with the Contract Documents and pro-actively manage critical path tasks; 
provide field support during testing phase; obtain completed works certification, attend 
substantial completion inspection and final walk-through; review punch list; prepare 
record drawings; and participate in closeout meeting with general contractor and the 
Town.  
 
Services shall also include support of the Town and Water District to ensure compliance 
of all contracts with terms and conditions of the grant package including submission of all 
required documentation to EFC to ensure compliance by all contractors and overall 
support required to the Town in meeting the established requirements.  
 
The services shall include, but not be limited to, the following: provide full time 
construction inspection during all active construction activity; maintain daily reports; 
review, evaluate and provide recommendations on change order proposals; monitor 
contractor’s general conformance with the Contract Documents; develop and maintain a 
punch list; participate in all final inspections; review and comment on Contractor payment 
requests; and participate in all meetings with contractor, Town and their representatives. 
 
2.  TERM OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement shall commence immediately upon execution of this Agreement by the 
respective parties and shall be completed in a timely manner. Notwithstanding the above, 
Town and Consultant agree that due to the nature of the Products (computer software, 
hardware, information technology) and the anticipated installation, implementation, and 
Page 59 of 284
 
 
integration of the various software and hardware modules with new and/or existing 
software, hardware etc. each of which may require different time frames and scheduling 
deadlines. Notwithstanding the above, the Town’s Financial Administrator and Consultant 
shall cooperate with each other and agree to the projected dates for installation, 
implementation, integration and training reflected in Section 3 “Payment I, II, and III”.  The 
services and licensing reflected in Annual Software Support and Licensing Agreement 
Schedule “B” are to be provided on an annual basis commencing after the first year (one 
year measured from date of installation).  
 
3.  PAYMENT 
For these services the Town will pay Consultant the price/payment listed on Schedule 
“A”.  
 
4.  RIGHTS TO DOCUMENTS OR DATA 
All information and data, regardless of form, generated in the performance of, or delivered 
under this Agreement, as well as any information provided to Consultant by Town, shall 
be and remain the sole property of Town.  Consultant shall keep all such information and 
data in confidence and not disclose or use it for any purpose other than in performing this 
Agreement, except with Town’s prior written approval. In the event that the legal right in 
any data and information generated in the performance of this Agreement does not vest 
in Town by law, Consultant hereby agrees and assigns to Town such legal rights in all 
such data and information.  Final payment shall not be due hereunder until after receipt 
by Town of such complete document and data file, or a certification that there is no such 
information created by the services performed under this Agreement, and receipt of all 
information and data which is the property of Town. These obligations shall survive the 
termination of this Agreement. 
5.  CONFIDENTIALITY 
Consultant acknowledges that it may have access to certain of Town’s confidential or 
work product documents for the purposes set forth in this Agreement. If any information 
or data is made available or accessible to Consultant, its employees, agents or 
contractors, pertaining to the Town’s business or financial affairs or to Town’s projects, 
transactions, Consultant will not store, copy, analyze, monitor or otherwise use that data 
except for the purposes set forth in the Agreement for the benefit of Town. Consultant will 
comply fully with all applicable laws, regulations, and use and maintain physical, technical 
and administrative safeguards relating to personally identifiable information (“PII”) and 
data privacy with respect to any such data that Consultant receives or has access to 
under the Agreement or in connection with the performance of any services for Town. 
Consultant will otherwise protect PII and will not use, disclose, or transfer across borders 
such PII except as necessary to perform under the Agreement or as authorized by the 
data subject or in accordance with applicable law. To the extent that Consultant receives 
PII related to the performance of the Agreement, Consultant will protect the privacy and 
legal rights of Town’s personnel, clients, customers and contractors. Consultant agrees 
to hold all such information confidential and to not disclose the same other than to the 
extent required to perform its duties, or upon approval by the Office of the Town Attorney-
written confirmation that it is appropriate for Consultant to respond or pursuant to a lawful 
court order. The requirements of this Section shall survive the termination of this 
Agreement.

6.  PUBLICITY 
Consultant shall not, without the prior written consent of the Town, provide, release or 
make available for inspection any documents, data, written material of any kind without 
the prior written consent of at least three members of the Town board or by resolution of 
the Town Board, except to the extent that Consultant determines it necessary to retain 
the services of a third party to assist in the preparation of the audit Consultant may release 
the documents, data, and such other written material provided said third party executes 
a confidentiality agreement in favor of the Town.   
            
7.  ASSIGNMENT AND SUBCONTRACTING 
Performance of any part of this Agreement may not be subcontracted nor assigned 
without, in each case, the prior written consent of at least three members of the Town 
Board or by resolution of the Town Board. 
 
8.  TERMINATION 
This Agreement may be terminated at any time by either party upon 30 days written notice 
to the other party.  In the event of such termination, Town shall have no further obligation 
to Consultant except to make any payments which may have become due under this 
Agreement.  
 
9.  RECORDS 
Consultant shall keep accurate records of the time spent in the performance of services 
hereunder.  The Town shall, until the expiration of seven years after final payment under 
this Agreement, have access to and the right to examine any directly pertinent books, 
documents, papers and records of Consultant involving transactions related to this 
Agreement. 
 
10.  CHANGES 
The Town, by resolution of the Town Board or written request by at least three members 
of the Town Board, within the general scope of this Agreement, may, at any time by written 
notice to Consultant, issue additional instructions, require additional services or direct the 
omission of services covered by this Agreement.  In such event, there will be made an 
equitable adjustment   in price and time of performance, but any claim for such an 
adjustment must be made within 15 days of the receipt of such written notice.  In the event 
that the Consultant determines that a change order is required, Consultant shall obtain 
written approval of the Town, by resolution or written consent of at least three members 
of the Town Board, and if the change shall require the payment of additional 
compensation, Consultant must obtain the written approval of three members of the Town 
Board or resolution of the Town Board for the additional compensation prior to 
commencement of work regarding the change order.  It is agreed and understood that no 
oral agreement, conversation, or understanding between the Consultant and the Town, 
its departments, officers, agents and employees shall effect or modify any of the terms or 
obligations of this Agreement or schedules annexed hereto and made a part hereof.

and regulations in the performance of its services under this Agreement.  Consultant will

indicted, suspended or debarred.  Consultant represents that Consultant has not been 
convicted of fraud of any other felony arising out of a contract with any local, state or 
federal agency.  In carrying out the work required hereunder, Consultant agrees not to 
make any communication to or appearance before any person in the executive or 
legislative branches of the local, state or federal government for the purpose of influencing 
or attempting to influence any such persons in connection with the award, extension, 
continuation, renewal, amendment or modification of any contract or agreement.  
Consultant may perform professional or technical services that are rendered directly in 
the preparation, submission or negotiation activities preceding award of a Town 
agreement/contract or to meet requirements imposed by law as a condition for receiving 
the award but only to the extent specifically detailed in the statement of work.  
Professional and technical services are limited to advice and analysis directly applying 
Consultant’s professional and technical discipline.

Consultant hereby represents and covenants that neither it nor any of its employees or

official, employee, representative shall receive either directly or indirectly anything of

contemplated application before any department of the Town, contract with the Town for 
sale of any product or service.  Consultant further represents and covenants that neither 
it nor any of its employees or representatives has offered or shall offer any gratuity to the 
Town, its officers, employees, agents or representatives with a view toward obtaining this 
Agreement or securing favorable treatment with respect thereto.  Consultant further 
represents that it will not engage in any activity which presents a conflict of interest in light 
of its relationship with Town.

16.  DISPUTES 
If Consultant fails to perform any of its obligations hereunder in accordance with the terms 
hereof, then after reasonable notice to Consultant not to exceed thirty (30) days, and an 
opportunity for Consultant to cure such failure (except in case of emergency), the Town 
may (but shall not be obligated to) cure such failure at the expense of the Consultant, and 
the amount incurred by the Town on demand.  Notwithstanding the above, any dispute 
arising under this Agreement which is not settled by Agreement of the parties may be 
settled by appropriate legal proceedings.  The Town and Consultant agree that prior to 
resorting to litigation, the matter be submitted to mediation upon the written request of 
either party and the results of such mediation shall only be binding upon agreement of 
each party to be bound thereby. The costs of mediation proceedings shall be shared 
equally by both parties. Pending any decision, appeal or judgment in such proceedings 
or the settlement of any dispute arising under this Agreement, Consultant shall proceed 
diligently with the performance of this Agreement in accordance with the decision of 
Town.

Re: Riverhead Water District 
Proposal for Engineering Services 
Extension No. 95 – River Road 
Installation of Water Mains & Appurtenances 
H2M Proposal No.: LP230059 
Dear Supt. Mancini: 
In accordance with the recent Request for Qualifications issued by the Town for engineering services associated 
with the installation of water mains and appurtenances, H2M is pleased to submit this proposal to provide 
professional engineering services related to the installation of a new water main along River Road in Calverton. 
Previous versions of this proposal included modified work scopes including engineering services for only a portion 
of the project extending from Edwards Avenue west as well as the inclusion of water mains along a private roadway 
connecting to River Road. The scope of work included under this proposal includes the entire length of River Road 
from Edwards Avenue to Connecticut Avenue as well as Private Road. 
It is our understanding that the Town of Riverhead has received grant funding through number of sources including 
the Bipartis an Infrastructure Law Emerging Contaminants (BIL -EC) as well as Congression al District Spending 
(CDS) Earmarks to help fund the new water main to extend service to approximately twenty -eight (28) dwellings 
along River Road, Railroad Avenue, Canoe Lake Drive and Private Road which receive potable water through 
private wells that either have been or are highly susce ptible to impact by perfluorinated compounds.    To provide 
public water service to these homes and in support of the Map & Plan dated July 2026, we recommend the following 
scope of work be included with the installation of new water mains: 
• Installation of approximately 12,000 linear feet of 6, 8” & 12” C.L.D.I. water main along River Road, Railroad
Avenue and Private Road incl. hydrant branches.
• Installation of twelve (12) fire hydrants.
• Directional drilling of the LIRR tracks on River Road in two locations.
• The crossing of Edwards Avenue shall be either by trenchless installation or open cut method.  It is known
that multiple gas and electric facilities exist within the right -of-way of Edwards Avenue. The most
appropriate installation method will be selected after reviewing the records for these utilities and review with
SCDPW personnel.
• Restoration of Town roads including a mill and overlay of half the road width where pavement is disturbed.
• Dewatering and removal of unsuitable material where groundwater is encountered during installation.
• Installation of a 1” water service up to the right-of-way line including tap, curb stop and meter pit to each lot
requesting to be hooked up to new water main.  It is not proposed to extend facilities onto private properties
of the individual homeowner under this contract.
The estimated cost to install the new water main in accordance with the above scope of work is $ 5,645,000. It 
should be noted this price includes costs associated with meeting requirements of the BIL -EC funding including 
American Iron & Steel (AIS), Build America Buy America (BABA), Davis-Bacon Federal Wages, and Disadvantaged 
Business Enterprises (DBE).  It is important to note that current market pricing is volatile, and the construction cost 
presented is based on the best information available at this time. With the current high inflation, supply chain 
interruptions and short supply of materials, prices presented are subject to change.

To continue to assist the District in this important project, H2M is proposing to provide the following engineering 
services: 
 
Task 1 – Preparation of Map & Plan 
• Already Completed. To be updated under Task 2. 
 
Task 2 – Map & Plan Revisions 
• Update Map & Plan at Town request to reflect revisions to cost, funding, scope and environmental review since 
initial issuance of report in March of 2021. 
 
Task 3 – NYSDEC & USEPA Environmental Permitting & Preliminary Grant Application Support 
• Prepare memorandum of Categorical Exclusion in accordance with National Environmental Policy Act (NEPA). 
• Assist Town with NYS Revolving Fund Equivalency checklist including coordination with Federal Agencies listed 
on Cross Cutter review check list including but not limited to SHPO, USFWS, Tribal Nation. 
• Prepare a NYSDEC Long Form and coordination with the Town of Riverhead on coordinated review to meet 
the requirements of SEQRA. 
• Delineate Tidal & Wetlands boundaries associated with Peconic River. 
• Prepare and submit application to NYSDEC for approval to construct new water line within and adjacent to 
State and Federal Regulated Freshwater Wetlands and within the Peconic River Wild, Scenic and Recreational 
River boundary. 
• NYSDEC Filing Fees shall be reimbursed by the Town at cost.  
• We propose to provide services associated with this task on an hourly rate basis.  
 
Task 4A - Planimetric Survey & Topography  
• Coordinate with other utilities including but not limited to Town of Riverhead Highway, Suffolk County, Long 
Island Railroad, and National Grid for attaining as-built information of utilities within the limits of the project. 
• Conduct detailed field investigation to locate all visible surface features such as road edges, driveways, utility 
features, fences, signs, etc. including existing water mains and marked utilities. Stand -alone trees will be 
identified, however wooded areas will be outlined. 
• Underground utilities will be marked to Quality Level “B” per ASCE guidelines. This includes mapping of 
information obtained through surface mark-out to obtain the horizontal position of existing utilities. Underground 
utility records will be requested of utility owners. 
• Survey of all storm facilities to determine inverts and pipe sizes. No confined space entry will be made.  
 
Task 5A – Design & Health Dept. Submissions 
• Prepare detailed design documents (plans and specifications) for the purpose of publicly bidding one 
construction contract for the Installation of Water Main & Appurtenances. Contracts shall include any 
requirements required for reimbursement through awarded grant including AIS, BABA, Davis-Bacon and DBE. 
• In accordance with New York State DOH requirements, the design shall include both a plan and profile of 
proposed water main. 
• Review documents with District personnel at 60% and 90% design and incorporate District comments into 
design documents. 
• Coordination with the MTA/LIRR for requirements concerning the crossing of railroad tracks in two locations. 
Request mark-out of LIRR facilities for inclusion on design drawings.  LIRR fees are not included in scope and 
shall be billed directly to Town/District as part of utility agreement. 
• Coordinate with Town Highway and Suffolk County road agencies to determine scope of final restoration on 
Town and County Roads. 
• Prepare the necessary applications and supporting documentation for submission to the Suffolk County and 
New York State Department of Health for review and approval of water supply improvement.  
• This fee is based on percent of construction using ASCE curve A.

construction period is estimated to be sixteen weeks.

Task 10 – Grant Support During Construction 
A requirement for accepting funding through the BIL-EC is to meet the requirements of the Environmental Facilities 
Corp. (EFC).  EFC requires the services provided for this project comply with the “Equivalency Project - Mandatory 
State Revolving Fund Terms & Conditions” for Construction & Non-Construction projects effective October 1, 2025. 
The grant stipulates that the overall work for all contracts (construction and non -construction) funded through the 
grant must meet program requirements. The services provided under this task include submission of all required 
documentation to EFC for all contracts to ensure compliance by all contractors and overall support required to the 
Town in meeting the established requirements.  As the extent of the support is not known, we propose to provide 
services associated with the oversight of documentation associated with the grant program on an hourly rate basis. 
 
H2M proposes to complete the above tasks in accordance with the agreed upon contract provided by the Town 
which shall include the “ Equivalency Project - Mandatory State Revolving Fund Terms & Conditions ” for Non -
Construction projects effective October 1, 202 5. We propose to perform the tasks associated with the project as 
described above in accordance with the following fee schedule:

Additional Services: 
The current engineering report for Extension No. 95 as well as the most recent grant application calls for the 
installation of water main along a private road that connects to River Road designated as S.C.T.M. designation 600-
144-01-017.6 & 017.7. As this is not a public right-of-way any water main installed within the limits of this road must 
be located within a water main easement dedicated to the Riverhead Water District. At this time the 
residents/owners of the road have not indicated their desire to connect to public water. In the event an agreement 
can be made with the property owners, the new water main on Private Road will be included in the scope of the 
project. To include the additional main, we recommend the following scope of work be included with the installation 
of new water mains: 
 
• Installation of approximately 1,000 linear feet of 8” C.L.D.I. water main along Private Road. 
• Installation of new fire hydrant. 
• Restoration of private road to include grading a placement of RCA or stone overlay. 
• Dewatering and removal of unsuitable material where groundwater is encountered during installation.  
• Installation of a 1” water service to each home emanating from proposed 8” water main.

owners, provide three signed and sealed copies of the document for the Towns use in filing with County.

are installed in correct location.

paid to employees while working in field.

are installed in correct location. 
• This project task is subject to prevailing wage rates as established by the NYS Department of Labor and will be 
paid to employees while working in field.

AUTHORIZES THE SUPERVISOR TO EXECUTE A LICENSE AGREEMENT WITH DARKSIDE 
PRODUCTIONS, INC. 2026 
 
Councilman Rothwell offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Kern 
 
          WHEREAS, the Town Board, by Resolution #2026-744, adopted on August 4, 2026, 
approved the Chapter 255 Application of Darkside Productions Inc. to conduct a haunted house 
walk-through, to be held at 5184 Rt. 25A Wading River, NY 11792, from September 25, 2026 
through November 1, 2026; and 
  
WHEREAS, Darkside Productions, Inc. has expressed a desire to enter into a license 
agreement with the Town of Riverhead to participate in a fire prevention/safety training course to 
be conducted by the Riverhead Fire Marshal, said course to provide a knowledgeable 
understanding on the origins of fires, sources of fires, how to protect oneself and others, how to 
prevent fires from occurring, fire emergency procedures, emergency exits and evacuations, to 
reduce the risk of potential injuries, death and property damage, in connection with the haunted 
house walk-through event; and   
  
WHEREAS, the Town Board desires to grant the license to Darkside Productions, Inc. for 
this event. 
  
          NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board hereby authorizes the 
Supervisor to execute a License Agreement between the Town of Riverhead and Darkside 
Productions, Inc. in connection with a fire prevention/safety training course to be conducted by 
the Riverhead Fire Marshal, to be held at 5184 Route 25A, Wading River, New York, on 
September 24, 2026; and be it further, 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a Certified copy of this 
resolution to the Fire Marshall and the Office of the Town Attorney; and be it further, 
  
RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a copy of same may be obtained 
from the Office of the Town Clerk.

AGREEMENT BETWEEN TOWN OF RIVERHEAD AND DARKSIDE PRODUCTIONS 
INC. FOR A FIRE PREVENTION/SAFETY TRAINING COURSE 
 
This Agreement is made and entered into as of this      day of September, 2026, 
by and between Darkside Productions, Inc., a corporation existing under laws of the State 
of New York, having a principal place of business at 4 Olive Street, Rocky Point, New 
York, 11778 and the Town of Riverhead, a municipal corporation with offices located at 4 
West Second Street, Riverhead, New York 11901. 
 
WHEREAS, Darkside Productions Inc., wishes to attend a Fire Prevention/Safety 
Training Course to be conducted by the Riverhead Fire Marshal on September 24, 2026, 
between the hours of 7:00 p.m. and 9:00 p.m. to take place at 5184 Rte. 25A, Wading 
River, the location being utilized for the haunted house walk-through events; and 
 
WHEREAS, the purpose of this Fire Prevention/Safety Course is to provide a 
knowledgeable understanding on the origins of fires, sources of fires, how to protect 
oneself, how to prevent fires from occurring, fire emergency procedures, emergency exits 
and evacuations, to reduce the risk of potential injuries, death and property damage. The 
Town of Riverhead has agreed to permit the Office of the Fire Marshal to conduct said 
Fire Prevention/Safety Training Course in connection with the haunted house walk-
through events; and 
 
WHEREAS, Darkside Productions Inc., agreed to terms under which it will 
participate in the Fire Prevention/Safety Training Course. 
 
NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants hereinafter set forth, 
the parties agree as follows:       
 
1.  Compensation:   Darkside Productions Inc., as required, will pay the Town $300.00 
for the payment of the attendance at the Fire Prevention/Safety Training Course to be 
conducted by the Riverhead Fire Marshal. This sum shall be made payable to the Town 
of Riverhead and shall be paid at the time Darkside Productions Inc. signs this 
Agreement.   
 
2. Insurance and Indemnification:  Darkside Productions Inc., will be responsible for 
providing commercial general liability insurance in the amount of not less than 
$2,000,000.00 with a company or companies reasonably satisfactory to the Town. 
Darkside Productions Inc. shall provide certificate(s) of the foregoing insurance, showing 
the Town of Riverhead as additional insured to the extent of their interest. Finally, 
Darkside Productions Inc. agrees to indemnify and hold the Town of Riverhead Boards, 
Departments and their respective officers, employees, agents, representatives and 
officials from any and all loss or liability associated with the events and related activities 
described herein, including actions, lawsuits, claims, liability for damages to property or 
for injuries or death to persons which may arise from, or be attributable or incident to the 
use by Darkside Productions Inc. and its employees, agents, representatives and 
concessionaires, except to the extent caused by the negligence or willful misconduct by 
Page 72 of 284
the Town of Riverhead. With respect to any suit or claim by Town of Riverhead whether 
under this indemnification provision or otherwise, Darkside Productions Inc. for itself, its 
agents, employees and representatives, hereby expressly waives any defense which 
might preclude or limit either enforcement of this indemnification clause or any reasonable 
outside attorney’s fees incurred by the Town securing compliance with the provision of 
this indemnification agreement. 
 
3. Successors and Assigns: This agreement shall be binding upon and inure to the 
benefit of the respective successors and assigns of the parties; provided, however, that 
nothing herein shall be deemed to permit the assignment of this Agreement by either 
party without the express written consent of the other party. 
 
4. Entire Agreement: This contract constitutes the entire agreement between the 
parties and no further agreement, express or implied, written or oral, exists with respect 
to the subject matter of this document. 
 
5. Governing Law: This Agreement shall be governed by and construed in 
accordance with the laws of the State of New York. 
 
In Witness Whereof, Darkside Productions Inc. has caused this instrument to be 
signed in its corporate name and Town of Riverhead has caused this instrument to be 
signed in its municipal name by Jerome Halpin, its Supervisor, hereunto duly authorized, 
as of the day and the year first above written. 
 
 
Darkside Productions Inc.

TB Resolution 2026-832 
 
ACCEPTS AND APPROVES THE APPRAISAL OF GOODMAN-MARKS ASSOCIATES, INC., 
ESTABLISHING JUST COMPENSATION AND AUTHORIZING THE TRANSMISSION OF A WRITTEN 
OFFER FOR THE ACQUISITION OF THE PROPERTY LOCATED AT 111 EAST MAIN STREET, 
RIVERHEAD, NEW YORK, SUFFOLK COUNTY TAX MAP # 129-1-11 
 
Councilwoman Merrifield offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilwoman Waski 
 
WHEREAS, the Town Board has commenced a condemnation proceeding in the Supreme 
Court of Suffolk County to acquire the property known as 111 E. Main Street, Riverhead, New 
York (hereinafter the “Subject Property”) which is currently owned by The Place For Learning, 
Inc. (hereinafter the “Record Owner”); and 
 
          WHEREAS, the Supreme Court has issued an Order on August 26, 2026 vesting title to 
the Subject Property in the Town of Riverhead and a copy of said vesting order is attached 
hereto; and 
 
          WHEREAS, pursuant to §303 of the New York Eminent Domain Procedures Law 
(hereinafter the “EDPL”), the Town Board is required to establish an amount it believes to 
represent just compensation for the Subject Property being acquired; and 
 
          WHEREAS, pursuant to EDPL §302, the Town Board retained the services of Goodman-
Marks Associates, Inc. whose members are licensed real estate appraisers in the State of New 
York, to evaluate and determine the fair market value of the Subject Property; and 
 
          WHEREAS, pursuant to EDPL §§303 and 304, the Town Board is required to make a 
written offer to the Record Owner to acquire the property for not less than one hundred per 
centum of the valuation established by the Town Board’s highest approved appraisal; and 
 
          WHEREAS, Goodman-Marks Associates, Inc. has completed a thorough, comprehensive 
valuation report dated June 2, 2026, concluding that the fair market value and just 
compensation for the acquisition of the Subject Property to be One Million Nine Hundred Fifty 
Thousand Dollars ($1,950,000.); and 
 
          WHEREAS, the Town Board has reviewed the appraisal, finds the methodology to be 
sound and hereby accepts and adopts Goodman-Marks Associates, Inc.’s valuation as its 
highest approved appraisal establishing just compensation of the Subject Property in 
accordance with EDPL §304; and 
 
          NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board hereby accepts, adopts, 
and approves the appraisal of Goodman-Marks Associates, Inc. and establishes the sum of 
$1,950,000.00 as the Town’s highest approved appraisal of just compensation for the 
acquisition of the Subject Property and hereby authorizes its Special Counsel, Frank A. Isler, to 
provide a written offer pursuant to EDPL §§ 303 and 304 to the Record Owner to acquire the 
property for the sum of One Million Nine Hundred Fifty Thousand Dollars ($1,950,000.); and be 
Page 79 of 284
it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that pursuant to EDPL §304 of the New York Eminent Domain Procedures 
Law, the written offer shall state that the Record Owner may accept the offer as payment in full 
or accept the offer as an advance payment without prejudice to the Record Owner’s right to 
litigate for additional compensation in the New York Supreme Court; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that the Town of Riverhead Financial Administrator hereby has the authority 
to establish the appropriate budget for the revenue and expenditure; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that the advanced compensation and final determination of compensation, if 
any, will be paid from the Town’s General Fund balance which fund is sufficient for such 
payment; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that the Town Supervisor, Town Attorney, and Special Counsel Frank A. 
Isler, are hereby authorized to execute any and all documents, agreements, or court documents 
necessary to effectuate this Resolution; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to forward a certified copy of this 
resolution to Special Counsel, Frank A. Isler (FIsler@sfliy.com), the Accounting Department and 
the Town Attorney; and be it further, 
 
          RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same may be 
obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk. 
 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: Adopted 
MOVER: Councilwoman Denise Merrifield 
SECONDER: Councilwoman Joann Waski 
AYES: Jerry Halpin, Kenneth Rothwell, Robert Kern, Denise Merrifield, Joann Waski 
NAYS: None

TB Resolution 2026-833 
 
ADOPTS BOND RESOLUTION FOR AMBULANCE BUILDING PROJECT 
 
Councilwoman Waski offered the following resolution,  
which was seconded by Councilman Rothwell 
 
At a meeting of the Town Board of the Town of Riverhead, in the County of Suffolk, New York, 
held at the Town Hall, 4 West Second Street, Riverhead, New York, on the 1st day of 
September, 2026. 
PRESENT: 
Jerome Halpin, Supervisor 
 Kenneth Rothwell, Board Member 
 Robert Kern, Board Member 
 Denise Merrifield, Board Member 
 Joann Waski, Board Member 
In the Matter of the 
Increase and Improvement of Facilities of the 
 Riverhead Ambulance District, in the  
 Town of Riverhead, in the County of Suffolk, New York, 
 pursuant to Section 202-b of the Town Law 
 
RESOLUTION AND 
 ORDER AFTER PUBLIC 
 HEARING 
WHEREAS, the Town Board has reviewed a report describing the proposed 
increase and improvement of facilities of the Riverhead Ambulance District (herein called the 
“District”), consisting of the construction of a new ambulance building to be located on the same 
site, 1111 Osborne Avenue, Riverhead, NY 11901 (Ambulance District Owned Property) 
SCTM# 600-108-2-5, as and in place of existing ambulance building, including any ancillary or 
related work in connection therewith, at the estimated maximum cost of $14,100,000; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, said report has been filed with the Town Board and the cost of said 
increase and improvement of facilities of the District has been estimated to be $14,100,000; and 
 
WHEREAS, the site of the new ambulance building is leased by the Town of 
Riverhead/Riverhead Ambulance District (Owner/Lessor) to the Riverhead Volunteer 
Ambulance District (Lessee), pursuant to a Lease Agreement by and between said parties (the 
“Lease Agreement”); and 
 
 
Page 84 of 284
WHEREAS, the new ambulance building shall be owned and retained by the 
District and the plan of financing said increase and improvement of facilities includes the 
expenditure of $6,000,000 provided by Riverhead Volunteer Ambulance Corp, as Lessee, from 
private funding secured by Lessee, and the expenditure of other available funds, with the 
remainder of said cost in the amount of not to exceed $8,100,000 to be financed by the 
issuance of bonds of the Town, with provisions set forth in said Lease Agreement providing for 
payment of amounts from the Lessee to the Lessor sufficient to pay debt service on the bonds; 
and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Town Board adopted an Order describing in general terms the 
proposed increase and improvement of such facilities, specifying the estimated cost thereof, and 
stating the Town Board would meet to hear all persons interested in said increase and 
improvement of facilities on September 1, 2026 at 2:00 P.M. (Prevailing Time) at the Town Hall, 
4 West Second Street, Riverhead, New York; and 
 
WHEREAS, a Notice of such public hearing was duly published and posted 
pursuant to applicable provisions of the Town Law; and 
 
WHEREAS, such public hearing was duly held by the Town Board on the 1st day 
of September, 2026 at 2:00 P.M. (Prevailing Time) at the Town Hall, 4 West Second Street, 
Riverhead, New York, with considerable discussion on the matter having been had and all 
persons desiring to be heard having been heard, including those in favor of and those in 
opposition to said increase and improvement of such facilities; 
 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to applicable provisions of the State Environmental 
Quality Review Act (SEQRA), constituting Article 8 of the Environmental Conservation Law, 
and 6 N.Y.C.R.R., Regulations Part 617, the Town Board has given due consideration to the 
proposed project described herein may have on the environment and and determined to be an 
Unlisted Action pursuant to the regulations of the New York State Department of 
Environmental Conservation promulgated pursuant to the State Environmental Quality Review 
Act ("SEQRA"), without need for coordinated review and no further review required. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT DETERMINED, that it is in the public interest to 
increase and improve the facilities of the District as hereinabove described, at the estimated 
total cost of $14,100,000; and it is hereby 
 
 
ORDERED, that the facilities of the District shall be so increased and improved 
and that a duly qualified engineer or architect shall prepare plans and specifications and make a 
careful estimate of the expense for said increase and improvement of such facilities and, with 
the assistance of the Town Attorney, shall prepare a proposed contract for such increase and 
improvement of facilities of the District, which plans and specifications, estimate and proposed 
contract shall be presented to the Town Board as soon as possible; and it is hereby, 
 
FURTHER ORDERED, that the expense of said increase and improvement of 
facilities shall be financed by the expenditure of $6,000,000 provided by Riverhead Volunteer 
Ambulance Corp, as Lessee, from private funding secured by Lessee, and the expenditure of 
other available funds, with the remainder of said cost in the amount of not to exceed $8,100,000 
to be financed by the issuance of bonds of the Town, and any costs of not covered by available 
funds, including payment of principal of and interest on said bonds, shall be subject to payment 
by the assessment, levy and collection of assessments upon the several lots and parcels of land 
Page 85 of 284
within the District; provided, however, that pursuant to the terms of the Lease Agreement, it is 
expected that funds received by the Town from the Lessee pursuant to the Lease Agreement 
shall be sufficient to pay debt service, including principal and interest, on the bonds; and it is 
hereby, 
 
FURTHER ORDERED, that the Town Clerk record, or cause to be recorded, a 
certified copy of this Resolution and Order After Public Hearing in the office of the Clerk of 
Suffolk County within ten (10) days after adoption thereof. 
DATED: September 1, 2026 
  
  
 (SEAL) TOWN BOARD OF THE TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
  
The adoption of the foregoing Resolution and Order was duly put to a vote on roll 
call, which resulted as follows: 
 Supervisor Jerome Halpin voting______________ 
 Board Member Kenneth Rothwell voting______________ 
 Board Member Robert Kern voting______________ 
 Board Member Denise Merrifield voting______________  
 Board Member Joann Waski voting______________ 
The Resolution and Order were declared adopted.

Independent Auditors’ Report 
 
 
The Honorable Supervisor and Town Board 
of the Town of Riverhead, New York 
 
Report on the Audit of the Financial Statements 
 
Opinions  
 
We have audited the accompanying financial statements of the governmental activities, the business-type activities, 
the aggregate discretely presented component units , each major fund and the aggregate remaining fund 
information of the Town of Riverhead, New York (“Town”), as of and for the year ended December 31, 2025, and 
the related notes to the financial statements, which colle ctively comprise the Town’s basic financial statements as 
listed in the table of contents. 
 
In our opinion, based on our audit and the reports of the other auditor, the accomp anying financial statements 
referred to above present fairly, in all material respects , the respective financial position of the governmental 
activities, the business-type activiti es, the aggregate discretely presented component units, each major fund and 
the aggregate remaining fund information of the Town, as  of December 31, 2025, and the respective changes in 
financial position and, where applicable, cash flows thereof  for the year then ended in accordance with accounting 
principles generally accepted in the United States of America. 
 
We did not audit the financial statements of the River head Industrial Development Agency and the Riverhead IDA 
Economic Job Development Corporation, which represen t 100% of both the assets and revenues of the aggregate 
discretely presented component units as of December 31, 2025, and the respective changes in financial position, 
and where applicable, cash flows ther eof for the year then ended.  Thos e statements were audited by another 
auditor, whose reports have been furnished to us, and our opi nions, insofar as they relate to the amounts included 
for the Riverhead Industrial Development Agency and the Riverhead IDA Economic Job Development Corporation, 
are based solely on the reports of the other auditor. 
 
Basis for Opinions 
 
We conducted our audit in accordance with auditing standar ds generally accepted in t he United States of America 
(“GAAS”) and the standards applicable to financial audits contained in Government Auditing Standards, issued by 
the Comptroller General of the United States.  Our respon sibilities under those standards  are further described in 
the Auditors’ Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Statements section of our report.  We are required to be 
independent of the Town, and to meet our other ethical resp onsibilities, in accordance with the relevant ethical 
requirements relating to our audit. We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and 
appropriate to provide a basis for our audit opinions. 
 
Responsibilities of Management for the Financial Statements 
 
Management is responsible for the preparation and fair pres entation of the financial statements in accordance with 
accounting principles generally accepted in the United St ates of America, and for the design, implementation, and 
maintenance of internal control relevant to the preparation and fair presentation of financial statements that are free 
from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.  
 
Page 96 of 284
 
- 4 - 
 
Responsibilities of Management for the Financial Statements (continued) 
 
In preparing the financial statements, management is re quired to evaluate whether there are conditions or 
events, considered in the aggregate, that  raise substantial doubt about the Town’s  ability to continue as a 
going concern for twelve months beyond the financ ial statement date, including any currently known 
information that may raise substantial doubt shortly thereafter. 
 
Auditors’ Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Statements 
 
Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the financial statements as a whole are free 
from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or erro r, and to issue an auditors’ report that includes our 
opinions.  Reasonable assurance is a high level of assu rance but is not absolute assurance and therefore is 
not a guarantee that an audit conducte d in accordance with GAAS and Government Auditing Standards  will 
always detect a material misstatement when it exists.  The risk of not detecting a material misstatement 
resulting from fraud is higher than for one resulting fr om error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, 
intentional omissions, misrepresentatio ns, or the override of internal control.  Misstatements are considered 
material if there is a substantial likelihood that, indivi dually or in the aggregate, they would influence the 
judgment made by a reasonable user based on the financial statements.  
 
In performing an audit in accordance with GAAS and Government Auditing Standards we: 
 
 Exercise professional judgment and maintain  professional skepticism throughout the audit.  
 Identify and assess the risks of ma terial misstatement of the financial statements, whether due to fraud 
or error, and design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks.  Such procedures include 
examining, on a test basis, evidence regard ing the amounts and disclosures in the financial 
statements.  
 Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant  to the audit in order to design audit procedures 
that are appropriate in the circ umstances, but not for the purpo se of expressing an opinion on the 
effectiveness of the Town's internal control.  Accordingly, no such opinion is expressed.  
 Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of significant 
accounting estimates made by management, as well as evaluate the overall presentation of the 
financial statements. 
 Conclude whether, in our judgement, there are conditions  or events, considered in the aggregate, that 
raise substantial doubt about the Town’s ability to continue as a going concern for a reasonable period 
of time. 
 
We are required to communicate with those charged wi th governance regarding, among other matters, the 
planned scope and timing of the audit, significant audit fi ndings, and certain internal control-related matters 
that we identified during the audit. 
 
Required Supplementary Information 
 
Accounting principles generally accepted in the Un ited States of America require that Management’s 
Discussion and Analysis and the schedules included under Required Supplementary Information in the 
accompanying table of contents be presented to supplement  the basic financial statements.  Such information 
is the responsibility of management and, although not a part  of the basic financial statements, is required by 
the Governmental Accounting Standards Board which considers it to be an essential part of financial reporting 
for placing the basic financial statements in an appropriate operational, economic, or historical context.  We 
have applied certain limited procedures to the required supplementary information in accordance with auditing 
standards generally accepted in the United States of America, which consisted of inquiries of management 
about the methods of preparing the information and co mparing the information for consistency with 
management’s responses to our inquiries, the basic financial statements, and other knowledge we obtained 
during our audit of the basic financial statements.  We  do not express an opinion or provide any assurance on 
the information because the limited pr ocedures do not provide us with su fficient evidence to express an 
opinion or provide any assurance.

statements.  Such information is the responsibility of management and was derived from and relates directly to 
the underlying accounting and other records used to prepar e the basic financial statements.  The information 
has been subjected to the auditing procedures applied in the audit of the basic financial statements and certain 
additional procedures, including comparing and reconciling such information directly to the underlying 
accounting and other records used to prepare the ba sic financial statements or to the basic financial 
statements themselves, and other ad ditional procedures in accordance  with auditing standards generally 
accepted in the United States of America.  In our opinion, the combining and individual fund financial 
statements and schedules are fairly stated, in all material respects, in relation to the basic financial statements 
as a whole. 
Other Reporting Required by Government Auditing Standards 
In accordance with Government Auditing Standards, we have also issued our report dated August 7, 2026 on our 
consideration of the Town’s internal control over financial reporting and on our tests of its compliance with certain 
provisions of laws, regulations, contracts and grant agreements and other matters.  The purpose of that report is 
solely to describe the scope of our testing of internal  control over financial reporting and compliance and the 
results of that testing, and not to provide an opinion on the effectiveness of the Town’s internal control over 
financial reporting or on compliance.  That report is an  integral part of an audit performed in accordance with 
Government Auditing Standards  in considering the Town’s internal control over financial reporting and 
compliance.

As management of the Town of Riverhead (the “Town”), we offer readers of the financial statements this narrative 
overview and analysis of the financial activities for the year ended December 31, 2025. Please read it in 
conjunction with the Town’s financial statements. 
 
FINANCIAL HIGHLIGHTS 
 
 The assets and deferred outflows of the primary gove rnment exceed its liabilities and deferred inflows of 
resources at the close of 2025 by $166,020,964 (net pos ition). The net position of governmental activities 
was $92,872,749 and the net position of business-type activities was $73,148,215. 
 
 The primary government’s total net position incr eased by $16,356,883 or 10.9%. The governmental 
activities net position increased by $11,607,173 and the business-type activities net position increased by 
$4,749,710. 
 
 The Town is committed to provide postemployment benef its to its employees in the form of pensions and 
healthcare. As a result, the Town has recognized subs tantial liabilities in the government-wide financial 
statements for these benefits. As of December 31, 2025, the Town had liabilities of $142,758,111 for other 
postemployment benefits recorded in accordance with  the provisions of Governmental Accounting 
Standards Board (“GASB”) Statement No. 75, “Accounting and Financial  Reporting for Postemployment 
Benefits Other Than Pensions” (“OPEB”), $8,416,727 for its proportionate share of the net pension liability 
of the New York State and Local Employees’ Retirement System (“ERS”), and $20,508,655 for its 
proportionate share of the net pension liability of the New York State and Local Police and Fire Retirement 
System (“PFRS”) recorded in accordance with the provisions of GASB Statement No. 68, “Accounting and 
Financial Reporting for Pensions” as of the plans’ year end of March 31, 2025. More detailed information 
about the Town’s pension and OPEB reporting is presented in Notes 3F and 3G in the notes to financial 
statements.  
 
 The Town adopted GASB Statement No. 102, “ Certain Risk Disclosures ,” for the fiscal year ended 
December 31, 2025. The adoption of this Statement did not have a material impact on the Town’s financial 
statements. 
 
 As of the close of 2025, the primary government ’s governmental funds reported combined ending fund 
balances of $77,788,396, an increase of $10,604,827 in comparison with the prior year. Of this amount, 
$2,137,276 is not in spendable form or is required to remain intact. The remaining $75,651,120 or 
approximately 97.3% of total fund balances is in sp endable form with various levels of spending 
constraint: restricted, committed, assigned, or una ssigned making them available for spending at the 
Town’s discretion.   
 
 At the end of 2025, the primary government’s tota l fund balance for the General Fund was $33,303,051, 
an increase of $4,899,127 or 17.2% from the prior year. The aggregate assigned and unassigned fund 
balance for the General Fund was $31,230,158, or 48.7% of total General Fund expenditures and other 
financing uses. 
 
 The primary government’s total bonded debt outstandi ng, inclusive of general obligation bonds and New 
York State Environmental Facilities Corporation bonds (“EFC bonds”), exclusive of premiums, was 
$38,423,858, a decrease of $5,536,040 or 12.6%. The Town’s governmental activities bonded debt 
outstanding decreased by $3,783,442 and the Town ’s business-type funds bonded debt outstanding 
decreased by $1,752,598.
